金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 7 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 荷姆茲海峽商船接連遇襲，美伊停火協議宣告終結：中東地緣政治局勢惡化，荷姆茲海峽商船
接連遇襲 ， 美方宣布結束停火並對伊朗境內伊斯蘭革命衛隊的防空與飛彈據點等多個目標發動
多輪空襲，同時撤銷其石油出口的制裁豁免，伊朗隨後採取報復並宣稱封鎖荷姆茲海峽，導致
海灣能源運輸再度受阻，帶動油價反彈。
◼ SK 海力士成功完成那斯達克掛牌：南韓半導體巨頭 SK 海力士以 ADR 形式登陸那斯達克(代
號：SKHY)，募資規模高達 265 億美元，為次世代 HBM 與先進封裝的擴產提供雄厚資金，募

集資金將挹注 HBM 與先進封裝產能，降低整體 AI 硬體供應鏈的斷鏈風險。美國 ADR 掛牌首
日大漲 13%後，引發 SK hynix 在南韓市場「利多出盡」賣壓，7/13 下跌 15.37%。
◼ 聯準會會議紀要出爐 ， 刻意減少前瞻指引 ：美國聯準會 6 月利率決策會議紀要顯示對今年利率
走向仍存在明顯分歧 ， 部分官員認為年底有機會降息 ， 惟另一部分官員則認為仍有進一步升息
之可能，紀要並未對任一方向表露傾向；點名 AI 基礎設施的強勁需求，可能持續對科技產品
與電力價格形成上行壓力；多數官員支持簡化 FOMC 會後聲明內容，認為現階段是檢討 Fed
對外溝通方式的適當時機。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ 美股短線聚焦財報表現：美股第二季財報季即將登場，大型金融機構將率先揭露營運成果，此
外，台積電法說(7/16)亦為科技股後續表現的關鍵觀察重點，其對 AI 需求及資本支出的看法
將影響市場對 AI 產業評價。區域市場方面，建議持續關注與AI 供應鏈連動度較高的台灣及日
本股市，其表現可望受惠於全球 AI 資本支出循環延續。

◼ 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：在未來聯準會減少前瞻指引、利率路徑更依賴數據
的環境下，市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將提高，債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人
對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較大波動；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政
府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等
債 ， 不僅能提供相對具吸引力的息收 ， 若未來通膨預期放緩 ， 在資本利得方面亦可望有所表現 。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 5 月通膨進一步攀升，個人消費支出(PCE)年增 4.1%，為三年來首次突破
4.0%，雖美國與伊朗在簽署初步和平協定後 ， 油價已回落至戰爭前水準， 通膨有
機會見頂，不過在 AI 熱潮推動下，相關科技商品價格上漲，可能抵銷油價下滑
的影響，市場預期聯準會可能於 9 月升息。
⚫ 美國財長貝森特 上周三(6/24)對聯準會主席華許减少前瞻性利率指引的計劃表
示贊賞， 也支持新主席不在季度經濟預測中提交點陣圖預測的決定， 但表示聯準
會官員需要對伊朗衝突對通膨的影響以及 AI 推動的生産率提升保持開放態度。
⚫ 歐洲央行總裁拉加德上周一(6/22)表示，歐元區面臨的通膨衝擊大到無法忽視，
但尚未嚴重到足以推高長期通膨預期或引發危險的第二輪通膨效應。儘管拉加
德未明確提及任何進一步舉措，但她的言論可能 強化市場對於歐洲央行進一步
緊縮的預期，目前市場已完全消化年底前歐洲央行再次升息的可能性。

⚫ 英國首相施凱爾上周一(6/22)宣布辭職，爲有序進行的權力移交鋪平道路，消息
公布後英鎊兌美元匯率止跌回穩， 英國公債價格也出現反彈 ，而接任呼聲最高人
選是同為執政黨工黨籍的國會議員柏南，最早可能於下個月成爲英國 10 年來的
第七位首相。
⚫ 中國央行上周一(6/22)公布 6 月貸款市場報價利率 （LPR） 連續第13 個月持穩，
1 年期 LPR 為 3.00%，5 年期以上 LPR 為 3.50%，均保持不變，符合市場調
查結果。
⚫ 泰國央行上周三(6/24)連續第二次會議維持指標利率在 1%不變 ， 符合市場預期 。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Jun/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.578，低點在 31.925，以 31.860 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 2 角 7.2 分。 
上週台幣走貶，週初，受到前周美國聯準會態度偏鷹的影響，強化市場對年內
升息的預期 ，美元指數延續前周走勢 ，連續三日走強，台幣開低走低，接連突破 31.600、
31.700 及 31.800 等多道整數關卡；臨近周末，美國公布 5 月 PCE 數據符合市場預
期，美元指數高檔震盪，不過外資上周三和周五分別爆出歷史最高和次高的賣超台
股紀錄 ，整週累積賣超 3,312 億並持續匯出，台幣承壓走貶 ，貶破 31.900 整數關口，
最低來到 31.925，終場央行調節之下收在 31.860。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣震盪偏貶。就國際市場而言，聯準會新主席華許釋出鷹派訊號，
且美國 5 月通膨數據仍高，強化市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期，加上美伊初步協
議前景仍具不確定性，皆支撐美元指數表現；就台灣市場來看，近期美國科技股大
幅回調引發股市震盪，也帶動台股自高點回落，在外資賣超台股並匯出之下，將令
台幣承壓，不過鄰近季底，且匯價已接近 32 整數關卡，預計逢高將吸引不少出口商
進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶幅，綜合以上，預估本週台幣震盪偏貶。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.100

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Jun/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走貶。週初，受到前周美國聯準會態度偏鷹的影響，強化市場對年
內升息的預期 ， 美元指數持續走強，境外人民幣走貶，最低來到 6.8194；臨近週末 ，
美國公布 5 月 PCE 數據 ， 符合預期 ， 美元指數高檔震盪 ，人民幣收斂部分週初跌幅，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.8048，週線貶值 221 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.7978，週線貶
值 250 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，且美國 5
月通膨數據仍高 ，強化市場對於 9 月升息的預期 ， 加上美伊協議前景仍具不確定性，
皆支撐美元指數表現，且周四將公布的美國 6 月非農就業數據，市場預計偏向謹慎
觀望；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價緩步走跌，不過在上周五暫時脫離近一個
月低點，顯示監管單位仍有意維持匯價穩定，預期將限制人民幣貶值的幅度；最後
就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線雖維持空頭排列，但USD/CNH 接
連向上突破月線及季線，並站穩季線之上，顯示先前的下行動能暫歇。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.7800-6.8300。

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         June/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲 。週初，美國 6 月製造業 PMI 優於市場預期，帶動 USD/JPY
最高來到 161.92；週間，美日兩國均公布通膨數據維持 3%以上高位，USD/JPY 大
致在 161.50 至 161.80 間盤整；臨近週末，日本公布 6 月東京核心 CPI 為 1.6%符合
市場預期，USD/JPY 維持區間走勢，終場收在 161.73，週線上漲 47 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本公佈 5 月企業服務價格
指數年增率為 3.3%，在貨物運輸價格飆升及住宿費上漲帶動下，指數連續3 個月達
3%以上水準。近期日本央行總裁植田和男亦強調通膨持續超過 2%目標，將持續升
息，預計將支撐日幣走勢。二、美國公布 5 月 PCE 年增 4.1%，剔除波動較大的能
源與食品價格的核心 PCE 年增 3.4%，爲 2023 年 10 月以來最高。此次數據雖符合
市場預期，惟漲幅持續顯示通膨壓力仍未獲明顯緩解，利率期貨市場顯示聯準會最
快將於今年 9 月升息一碼，美元持穩，將限縮日幣走升空間。三、就技術面而言，
USD/JPY 各天期均線向上發散，盤勢偏多，惟匯價在突破 161 高位後多次以十字線
做收，顯示上行動能暫歇。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現高檔震盪的可能性較
高。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：160.50-162.70 
前週交易區間 
161.06 161.94

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
            June/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元區 6 月製造業 PMI 低於市場預期，歐元走弱
至 1.14 附近；週間，美國公布通膨數據偏高，聯準會升息預期升溫，美元走強，帶
動歐元最低來到 1.1324；臨近週末，美國公布 6 月消費者信心指數低於預期，歐元
小幅回升，終場收在 1.1383，週線下跌 82 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、美國公布第一季 GDP 成長
2.1%，高於市場預期，6 月製造業 PMI 為 55.7 位於榮枯線之上，另初請失業金人數
降至 21.5 萬人，亦優於市場預期，數據顯示美國經濟穩健、就業具韌性，加上通膨
仍維持高位等各項條件下，皆有利於聯準會持續維持緊縮政策，市場對聯準會今年
升息預期升溫，預計將不利歐元走勢。二、歐洲央行執委施納貝爾(Isabel Schnabel)
表示，鑒於能源價格仍高，依目前情勢歐洲央行仍需要進一步升息，以在中期內將
通膨拉回至 2%的目標水準 ，市場預期歐洲央行年底前將再升息一碼 ， 預計將限縮歐
元下行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 短中天期均線向下發散走勢
偏空，惟匯價在多次下探 1.1330 附近均拉回，顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上，
預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1280-1.1510 
前週交易區間 
1.1324 1.1473

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 06.22-115.06.26 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.64% ~ 1.66% 
公債－10 年期- A15106 1.690% 
    － 5 年期- A15101     1.555%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，上周央行會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合市場預
期 ， 本周銀行間資金分配略為不均 ， 惟供給尚屬平穩 ， 令短率持穩於近期區間 。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.64%~1.66%，利率高位較上週下跌 2 個基本點。

二、 國際油價下滑帶動國際債市殖利率回落，惟在貨幣市場短率仍處於相對高位
下，台債交投仍偏冷清，本週指標券皆未成交，5 年券 A15101 及 10 年券
A15106 週五參考報價利率分別為 1.555%、1.690%。 
臨近季底且台股交易熱絡鎖住資金，加上外資大量的匯出也令貨幣市場趨於
緊俏 ， 故在資金面尚未轉向寬鬆下 ， 預料台債殖利率維持高檔區間盤整 ， 預估
5 年券交易區間在 1.62%~1.63%、10 年券交易區間在 1.72%~1.73%。

Update
E Ink Holdings Inc.  | Asia Pacific
June  Sales +23% MoM/-1% YoY
In this report, we focus on E Ink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• June sales were NT$4,041mn (+23% MoM/-1% YoY).
• 2Q26 sales came in at NT$10,216mn (+18% QoQ/-4% YoY), largely in line
with our estimate at NT$10,043mn (+16% QoQ/-5% YoY)  and the consensus
at NT$10,227mn (+18% QoQ/-4% YoY).
Our view:
• Management retained its guidance of revenue to grow by 20-25% in 2026,

driven by 20-30% growth from ESLs, single-digit % growth from e-readers/e-
notes and high-single-digit % of revenue contribution from signage (mostly
retail media and indoor applications).
• That said, the potential impact to the end demand for its e-readers/e-notes
from the rising memory costs is worth monitoring.
• Longer term, we continue to believe in secular  demand in e-paper from
various applications and E Ink's dominant to ride on the trend

• Stay OW with PT of NT$250 (19x 2027 P/E vs. currently at 15x).

Valuation Methodology and Risks
E Ink Holdings Inc. (8069.TWO)
Base case, 19x 2027e P/E. We believe this valuation methodology best captures E Ink's earn-
ings growth momentum. We view our target multiple of 19x as fair, considering our estimated
20% earnings CAGR for 2025-28. The implied PEG of 1.0x is slightly higher than its typical level
in 2020-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 21x one-year forward consensus

EPS with a 24% earnings CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Firmer-than-expected ESL orders
n Stronger demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Better macro conditions
n Milder competition
n Brisker inventory digestion
Risks to Downside
n Softer-than-expected ESL orders
n Slower demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Macro headwinds
n Intensified competition
n More protracted inventory digestion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

蔡博任 
02-27815995#37122 
pojen.tsai@fubon.com 
中磊 (5388 TT)

2Q26 營收優於預期

維持買進評等，目標價 106 元，潛在上漲空間 27% 
1. 2Q26 營收優於預期 
2. 今年成長動能來自企業與家用網通設備升級 
3. 記憶體成本已順利轉嫁

2Q26 營收優於預期 
中磊 6 月營收 72.7 億元 ， 月增12%， 年增71%，2Q26 營收 206 億元 ， 季增24%，
年增 69%，優於預期，主因今年北美電信公司的光纖產品與 5G FWA 開始大量出
貨，將是成長幅度最大的區塊，預估毛利率 13.4%，上修 EPS 預估為 2.1 元。

今年成長動能來自企業與家用網通設備升級 
今年成長動能將來自 1)企業與家用寬頻設備升級：AI 應用的興起導致數據量暴增 ，
進而帶動基礎建設必須隨之升級，2026 年將是 Wi-Fi 7 技術大規模導入的一年、
DOCSIS 4.0 與 10G PON 升級、以及 5G FWA 需求持續放量，北美電信運營商營
收增加。2) 分散式局端 DAA 設備開始出貨，因應 Cable 技術升級的關鍵產品，旨
在減少訊號衰減與延遲，雖然目前認證進度略有延遲，但預計 2026 年下半年相關
產品開始貢獻營收，預計明年將有更大量貢獻，整體市場潛在規模可達數百億元。

記憶體成本已順利轉嫁 
估計記憶體成本佔比已達雙位數百分比。DRAM/Flash 面臨嚴重短缺，8GB DDR4
的現貨價已從原本約幾塊美元飆升至數十美元以上，中磊已順利將增加的成本轉嫁
給客戶，由於電信商客戶的核心商業模式是賺取用戶月費，流失訂戶的損失遠大於
設備成本漲價，因此轉嫁成功率高，雖然記憶體漲價會因分母變大而稀釋毛利率，
但隨著營收規模擴大與費用率收斂，營業利益率上升，估計整體獲利的成長幅度將
會超越營收的成長幅度。

調升今明兩年獲利預估 
因 2Q26 營收優於預期，我們調升 2026、2027 年 EPS 預估至 7.05 元、7.09
元，維持買進評等，目標價 106 元，約為 2027 年 PER 15x。

富邦不同之處 
市場 Consensus 預估 2026、2027 年 EPS 為 6.21 元、8.46 元。

評價與風險 
中磊為台灣大型網通業者，主要同業為智易(3596 TT) 、啟碁(6285 TT) ，大型網通
族群本益比區間為 12~20 倍。主要投資風險為：(1)戰爭造成全球景氣衰退、(2)電
信標案容易帶來較大幅度地營收起伏 、(3)晶片成本高漲或供應鏈吃緊等因素容易造
成獲利空間被壓縮。

富邦的股票評等標準 
評等  定義 
買進 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬超過15%  
中立  預估未來6個月內有絕對報酬介於15%與負15%之間 
賣出 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬高於負15% 
未評等 由於富邦目前與該公司有特定交易或沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等  
評估中 目前正在研議個股的投資評等，將於3到6個月內提供投資評等

產業評等 定義 
優於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現突出 
持平 預估該產業在未來6個月內與大盤指數表現相較持平 
劣於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現較差

This report has been produced in Chinese. In the event of questions regarding the accuracy of the translation, please refer t o the original Chinese version.

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months  
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,720,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.07.08) 
1,041,000 
방위산업

 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,720,000원으로 하향 
 컨센서스 대비 매출 부합, 영업이익 하회 전망 
 26년 상저하고 실적 전망, 하반기 집중되는 수주 모멘텀

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,720,000원으로 하향 
26년 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 31.3x 적
용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 목표주가 하향은 연결 자
회사 가치 하락에 따른 조정으로, 동사의 적극적인 현지화 전략을 통해 업
종 내 시장 점유율 지속 확대를 전망하며 업종 내 Top-Pick 유지

컨센서스 대비 매출액 상회, 영업이익 하회 전망 
2Q26 연결 기준 매출액 8.4조원(+33.6% YoY), 영업이익 9,440억원(-
23.6% YoY, OPM 11.2%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.2조원 상회, 영업이익 
1.0조원 하회 전망. 폴란드향 매출 인식이 부재했던 1Q26와 달리 폴란드 
천무 매출이 호주, 이집트 K9 자주포 매출과 함께 반영되며 수출비중은 
1Q26 53.3%에서 2Q26 65.8%로 상승, 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 지상방산 영
업이익률 또한 1Q26 16.9%에서 2Q26 24.9%로 증가할 것이라 추정

26년 상저하고 실적 전망, 하반기 집중되는 수주 모멘텀 
2H26 호주 K9 자주포 매출 및 폴란드 K9 자주포, 천무 매출이 집중됨에 
따라 3Q26 수출 비중은 75.1%로 연내 가장 높은 수출 비중 및 영업이익
률을 보일 것이라 추정. 26년 수주 공백기가 길어지고 있지만 여전히 시
장에서 기대하고 있는 사업들은 문제없이 진행되고 있으며 이란-미국 전
쟁 종전에 따라 지연되었던 사업들이 가속화되며 실적과 수주 모멘텀이 
동반되는 하반기를 전망 
CPSP 사업을 통해 확인한 바와 같이 현 시점 방위산업 외형 확장에 가장 중
요한 것은 현지화 전략. 동사의 적극적인 현지화 전략은 단기간 내 실적으로 
성과를 보이지 않을 수 있지만 폴란드, 루마니아, 미국, 호주 내 이뤄졌던 현
지 투자들은 중장기적으로 실적으로 돌아오는 것은 물론 향후 수주 가능성
을 한 단계 더 높여 주는 마중물 역할을 수행할 것이라 전망

Document 293: FX Daily_ The big GBP short unwinding continues

### Page 1

Article | 16 July 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  16 July 2026   FX
FX Daily: The big GBP short unwinding
continues
The dollar stayed under pressure as PPI confirmed that inflation pressure
eased in June. Still, with the Gulf situation apparently far from de-
escalation, we don’t see much more USD downside potential in the near
term. Instead, sterling is emerging as a major outperformer on the back
of further positioning adjustments

News that Shabana Mahmood could become Andy Burnham’s chancellor has boosted the pound
USD: Losing more ground
The dollar has remained under pressure, with FX volatility resuming its decline after a short-
lived bounce earlier this week. Brent settling at around $85/bl is seemingly not enough to drive
inflation expectations much higher, and the USD front-end continues to feel some gravitational
pull from soft June CPI data. PPI was also rather muted yesterday: -0.3% MoM for headline,

0.2% MoM for core.
At the same time, the two-day testimony by Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and a speech by Chris
Waller are warning markets against reading too much into one single inflation print. According
to Waller, the disinflationary trend must be visible over a few months to call off hikes. Hardly a
guarantee now that oil prices have risen again.

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 16 July 2026 2
All in all, markets may remain content with one Fed hike priced in this year. As some other
central bank pricing catches up on the upside, the dollar might still be inching lower in most
crosses.
US retail sales for June are published today, and are expected to grow at 0.2% MoM after four
very strong months. The Fed’s Logan and Schmid (both hawks) are scheduled to speak.
Francesco Pesole

EUR: Rally should lose steam
We aren't convinced this EUR/USD rally has much further to run, barring clear signs of a de-
escalation in the Middle East. The rise in gas prices (more impactful for the eurozone's terms of
trade than crude) should prevent the euro from building any idiosyncratic narrative at this
point.
We expect EUR/USD sellers to emerge around the 1.1500 area, and see a greater chance of
rangebound stabilisation at this stage rather than a break higher. The eurozone calendar is

empty today, with the ECB entering its pre-meeting quiet period.
Francesco Pesole
GBP: Shorts on the run
The sterling short squeeze continues. The catalyst for EUR/GBP to break below 0.8500
yesterday was reports that Shabana Mahmood would be Andy Burnham’s pick for chancellor
when he likely comes to power next week. Mahmood is seen to the right of the Labour Party
and a less divisive – and potentially less fiscally expansive – candidate for chancellor than Ed

Miliband. 10-year UK gilts outperformed German Bunds by around 5bp yesterday.
The strength of the sterling rally looks more a function of position adjustment rather than a
massive re-assessment of the prospects for UK PLC, however. Positioning data from US futures
exchanges had recently shown speculators running the shortest sterling positions since 2017.
It may be difficult to stand in the way of the sterling rally in the short term, where a sustained

break of 0.8470 opens up 0.8400 for EUR/GBP, while GBP/USD could make a run at
1.3600/3650. And next week looks a big one for UK inputs, with Burnham taking the reins and
both CPI and jobs data released.
Yet when position-adjustment activity fades and investors return to the UK macro/Bank of
England story, EUR/GBP should be able to retest the recent break-out area around
0.8600/8610. That may be a story for much later this month.
Chris Turner

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 16 July 2026 3
CEE: Stabilisation should bring buyers back to region
The CEE region remains in the grip of global events, with a dovish shift in Fed pricing on one
hand and still elevated oil prices on the other. We may see some relief in the region for the
second day in a row, but the risks are not changing much for now. However, the worst of the
US-Iran escalation now appears to be behind us, barring any fresh developments. This could

attract more interest in CEE FX in the event of another day of stability and take advantage of
cheaper levels, especially in the Hungarian forint, which remains at the upper edge of our
350-360 EUR/HUF range.
Today's data in the region has little chance of changing anything with the dominant global
story. In Hungary, wages for May will be published, which remain elevated and the highest
within the CEE region, and at the same time, one of the few upside risks for the inflation profile.

Document 304: Italy’s productivity challenges_ can AI provide a solution_ (2026-07-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Italy’s productivity challenges_ can AI provide a solution_ (2026-07-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-07-01
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

1st July 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
In the years following the pandemic, labour productivity in Italy has stalled. Artificial intelligence is identified as a potential catalyst for reversing this
trend, with projections indicating possible annual productivity growth increases of up to 1.1 p.p. in a scenario of rapid adoption. However, the actual
adoption of AI in Italy is still low, despite a faster growth rate compared with its main Euro area counterparts. As of 2025, only 16.4% of Italian com-

panies with more than 10 employees were using AI. In the financial and insurance sectors, adoption rates are above average (39%, peaking at 70% in
insurance). However, the application remains largely superficial, primarily focused on support activities such as fraud detection, process automation
and customer relations, while its use in trading and post-trading activities remains largely experimental.
Adopting AI could improve labour productivity. In the years after the pandemic, the dynamics of labour productivity in Italy have been disappointing.

When measured as output per hour worked, there was a decline of 4.7% between 2020 and 2025, compared to a -1% decrease in France, a +0.9% increase
in Germany and a +2.5% increase in Spain. Although there has been some improvement in recent years – with a more limited contraction of -1.9% during
the 2023-2025 period – Italy’s productivity dynamics remain weaker compared to the stagnation or gains observed in other major European economies
(Germany +0.1%; Spain +1.9%; France +1.5%).

The spread of artificial intelligence is highlighted as a factor that could reverse this trend. Estimates by Filippucci et al. (2025)1 suggest that over the next
decade, AI adoption could deliver annual labour productivity growth gains ranging from 0.2 to 0.8 p.p., particularly benefiting countries experiencing signi-
ficant demographic declines, such as Japan and Italy. In the United States, where the bulk of global investment in AI is currently concentrated2, the annual

increase in labour productivity growth could reach 1.3 p.p.
If the adoption of AI were to proceed rapidly, the potential benefits for Italy could be significantly greater, adding as much as 1.1 p.p. to productivity
growth. A recent analysis3 identifies the sectoral contributions to GDP growth under a scenario of rapid adoption, differentiating between direct effects
(the productivity gains generated within each sector), and indirect effects (which arise through input-output linkages along the production chain).

Manufacturing stands out as the single largest contributor, adding approximately 2 p.p. to GDP growth over the decade, followed by Wholesale and
Retail trade (around 1 p.p.) and Professional services (around 0.8 p.p.), while the Financial and Insurance sector would account for a further 0.6 p.p.
1 Filippucci, F. et al. (2025), “Macroeconomic productivity gains from Artificial Intelligence in G7 economies”, OECD Artificial Intelligence Papers, No. 41, OECD Publishing, Paris.

2 The disparity in investment amounts is remarkable; between 2013 and 2025, the United States invested around USD 753 bn in AI, compared to USD 17.2 bn in Germany and
USD 15.6 bn in France, the only two Euro-area countries among the top ten global investors; see AI Index Report, 2026.
3 3 Fabio Bertolotti, Andrea Linarello, Patrick Zoi; Shock propagation and economic policies in the Italian production network; Banca d’Italia; June 2026.
ITALY’S PRODUCTIVITY CHALLENGES: CAN AI PROVIDE A SOLUTION?

Paola Verduci
Italy: reasons for not adopting AI, by firms’ size

SOURCE: BANCA D’ITALIA, 2026CHART 1

### Page 2

2
La banque
d’un monde
qui change
Chart of the Week / 1st July 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The magnitude of each sector’s contribution ultimately depends on three factors: its share of total value added, its direct exposure to AI, and its importance
within the production network.
AI adoption varies greatly from one sector to another. The intensity of AI adoption is influenced by various factors, including sectoral composition, the size
of companies and the availability of the skills needed to use it. Eurostat data shows the considerable heterogeneity in AI adoption among countries: despite

an increase of over 100% from 2024, by 2025 only 16.4% of Italian companies with over 10 employees were using AI, a figure comparable to France’s (18.2%)
but significantly lower than Germany’s (around 26%) and Spain’s (20.2%). Adoption rates increase steadily with company size: in the first half of 2026, 32%
of Italian companies with over 20 employees were using AI, a percentage that climbed to 63% among those with over 500 employees but fell to 28% among

smaller companies with 20-49 employees. A similar disparity is observed across sectors, with take-up comparatively limited in Manufacturing (27%) and
considerably more widespread in Services (36%). The “Other services” segment, which includes professional, scientific and technical activities, records the
highest average adoption rate, facilitated by a more uniform distribution of the technology across different company-size categories.
Italian companies cite a lack of skills as one of the main reasons for not adopting AI, particularly among small companies (10-49 employees). Additionally,

high costs, data availability and regulatory uncertainty also hinder adoption (see Chart 1).
Access aside, the ability to use the technology is equally important: the IMF’s Skill Readiness Index, which measures how prepared human capital is to
work with AI, places Italy towards the lower end of a ranking led by Ireland, Finland, Denmark and Switzerland. France and Spain rank slightly better than
Italy, while Germany occupies a marginally higher position.

In Italy, a recent survey4 indicates that companies in the Financial and Insurance sector adopt AI more widely than the average productive system (39%),
peaking at 70% in the insurance segment (see Chart 2). However, similar to their OECD counterparts, Italian financial institutions primarily use the tech-
nology for activities that lie outside the core functions of financial markets, such as fraud detection and prevention. This somewhat superficial application

reflects a cautious approach, mirroring trends observed in other OECD countries, where companies typically focus AI efforts on customer relations, process
automation, cybersecurity and fraud prevention, while applications in trading and post-trading remain largely experimental.5
Despite this, the benefits reported thus far are significant: among Italian financial companies using AI, three-quarters report improvements in operational
efficiency, almost two-thirds cite productivity gains, and roughly half report greater effectiveness in decision-making. However, gains in genuinely mar -

ket-relevant areas are still under-represented, illustrating a pattern of broad adoption that lacks depth in core market functions.
.
4 OECD/Banca d’Italia; Artificial Intelligence in Italian Financial Markets; June 2026. The 450 responses represent 49% of the entities supervised in Italy (including foreign compa-
nies operating in Italy under the supervision of Banca d’Italia). The question on AI adoption was mandatory. All figures are unweighted counts of responding entities by segment,

although coverage varies (e.g. 60% for banks; 48% for pension funds and 22% for securities investment firms).
5 In the United States, almost all financial functions (U.S. Department of the Treasury, 2024); in the United Kingdom, companies focus on optimising internal processes, enhancing
cybersecurity and detecting fraud (BoE/FCA, 2024); Swedish and Finnish companies prioritise summarisation, translation and automation (Finansinspektionen, 2024 and FIN-FSA,

2025); Japanese companies give priority to customer relations and targeting (Bank of Japan, 2024); Dutch banks concentrate on creditworthiness and fraud prevention (De Neder-
landsche Bank/AFM, 2024); French respondents report internal applications with no specific uses in financial activities (AMF, 2026).

2 Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Luke Templeman, Adrian Cox, Helen Beloposky, Miha Hribernik, Jim Reid | Macro, Markets, Geopolitics and Tech: A Guide for CFOs and Corporates | 13 July 2026
Introduction: Macro, Markets, Geopolitics & T ech
A guide for CFOs & Corporates
We're pleased to share our July 2026 Guide for CFOs and

Corporates, providing essential insights into macroeconomics,
markets, geopolitics and technology. This report offers critical
analysis and perspectives to help corporate clients navigate today's
complex economic environment.
Key highlights:
— Macroeconomic outlook: Resumption of US-Iran attacks in the
Strait has reignited fears of an oil-driven energy shock. So far, the
global economy has proved to be resilient.
— Market analysis: Corporate earnings growth is expected to be

strong and credit spreads have proved resilient to several shocks.
Brent crude averages $80/bbl in 2026 ($75 in Q3).
— Geopolitics: Corporates continue to cite geopolitics as a key risk,
although other ‘normal’ risks are high priorities. Q3/Q4 events to
watch: expiry of US global 10% Section 122 tariffs in Jul,
expiration of original US-Iran ceasefire in Aug, US midterms in
Nov.
— Artificial Intelligence: AI adoption is increasing, but corporates

cite challenges in scaling across business functions. Disruption
worries are relatively latent. The costs around AI capex demand
are shifting into the spotlight.
— Monetary policy: The energy shock is influencing central bank
trajectories, with both the Fed and ECB expected to hike rates.
Momentum around policymakers considering tokenization of
assets is growing.
Source: The House View: Infographic - 2026 Global Macro Events Calendar – 06-Jan-2026

Upcoming key macro events for rest of 2026

Three key risks for the global economy
Collapse of the Iran ceasefire is renewing stagflation concerns, while cybersecurity risks have become a

key concern for corporates
2. AI disruption
— Concerns that AI will disrupt the labor force have yet to be
borne out by the data. CEOs see little impact on jobs in the
near term, while companies struggle to diffuse adoption.
— Central banks are becoming more attuned to AI as a source
of upwards price pressure.
1. Collapse of Iran ceasefire
— Renewed flare-ups in the Middle East are driving oil prices
higher again, compounded by energy infrastructure attacks

between Russia and Ukraine. Corporates remain concerned
with economic conditions vs six months ago.
— Long term, however, the global economy seems more
resilient to geopolitical shocks.
3. Hawkish central bank trajectories
— Expectations for higher US rates will grow as markets absorb
the apparent demise of the ceasefire. Rates expectations had
already decoupled from oil prices even during the ceasefire.
— Expectations for ECB rate hikes have also shifted due to the

energy shock.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, The Conference Board Measure of CEO Confidence in collaboration with The Business Council

9 Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Luke Templeman, Adrian Cox, Helen Beloposky, Miha Hribernik, Jim Reid | Macro, Markets, Geopolitics and Tech: A Guide for CFOs and Corporates | 13 July 2026
Midterm Moderation: Will Trump Turn Dove?
Trump has a strong incentive not to shock markets or stoke inflation ahead of the vote in
November. But markets should expect more policy volatility, not less, as we move into

the second half of Trump’s term. An administration looking to demonstrate strength
through quick wins may pursue other issues more aggressively. Investors should watch
rising US pressure on Cuba and the political relationship with Europe as possible sources
of nearer-term movement.
The July expiry of Section 122 tariffs and their likely replacement by Section 301 levies
on dozens of trading partners should keep pressure on countries to strike bilateral deals

favourable to the US, spanning trade, investment, industrial and technology concessions.
In addition, Section 232 tariffs could expand into new categories.
While EU-US trade relations are more stable, there remains a tail risk that a transatlantic
security or sovereignty dispute (e.g. renewed American claims on Greenland) spills over
into the economic relationship. At the 7-8 July NATO summit, Trump reinforced his
expectations that European allies carry more of the security burden and his

disappointment in their lack of support for US efforts in Iran. Trump also reiterated his
continued interest in acquiring Greenland.
Trump will likely renew his push for a ceasefire in the Russia-Ukraine war. In early July,
Trump suggested “we’re getting much closer than people realize.” While a ceasefire this
year is far from guaranteed, any deal brokered by the Trump administration could offer
Russia sanctions relief, access to frozen assets and an expectation of an accelerated

Ukrainian accession to the EU.
What happens after the midterms actually matters more. Trump may become a “foreign
policy president” ,focused on cementing his legacy. This will include: embedding existing
tariff and investment deals with key US partners; deepening US leadership in the
Americas; creating the conditions for a pull-back of American resources from Europe;
and broadening US efforts to reduce rare-earths and critical-minerals dependence on

China.
Source: Library of Congress on Unsplash

Other geopolitical flashpoints
Key topics to monitor during 2026-Q3
— Iran: The 60-day limit for US-Iranian MOU (focusing on Iran’s nuclear programme and deal

implementation) will expire on 20 AUG but negotiations will almost certainly run past the initial
60 days. A significant escalation on over the past week saw the US launch strikes on hundreds
of targets across Iran and reimpose sanctions on Iranian oil sales in retaliation for Iran striking
ships in the Strait of Hormuz. Iran has fired a number of waves of strikes against Gulf nations.
While Trump has suggested that the ceasefire is over, we retain our assessment that both

parties are incentivized to find a near-term resolution as kinetic attacks will not drive a
resolution. That said, we should prepare for more volatility in the coming months and a
persistent threat to the Strait of Hormuz. The Middle East will likely remain a focus for the rest
of Trump’sterm, even if markets move on.
— China-US Relations: Post-summit strategic stability could deliver tactical trade and investment
gains and slow the pace of escalation into late 2026. But corporates and investors should still

prepare for a start-stop cycle of engagement and restriction as technology, tariffs and
economic security remain central to national security agendas in Beijing and Washington. A key
watchpoint will be Xi’s expected visit to the US on 24 September, just over a month before the
US midterm election set to be dominated by voter cost-of-living concerns. This could
incentivize the Trump administration to seek an economic agreement at the summit. An
extension of last October’s Busan agreement and an operationalization of the potentially

commerce-boosting US-China Board of Trade – which China hopes could include a ten-fold
increase in tariff-free trade – are possible deliverables.
— What next for Europe? An unresolved war in Ukraine, greater geopolitical fragmentation, and a less
predictable transatlantic relationship. At the same time, the geopolitical landscape is presenting myriad
opportunities – increased defence investment and a chance to build more strategic autonomy. There is

a growing recognition that the EU needs to overcome its often slow, consensus-driven decision-making
processes to respond more effectively to evolving geopolitical dynamics. The key question will be
whether Europe can convert these pressures into coordinated action, or whether internal divisions
stymie real progress.
For more on geopolitical flashpoints to watch out for, read our recent publications here: Geopolitical Outlook 2026: At the Midpoint, This Month in Geopolitics: July 2026,

Geopolitics beyond Iran, The Dragon and the Eagle: Decoding China-US Ties After Beijing
Source: IStock ugurhan

Selected geopolitical events in July - September
— Mid-July: UK: Andy Burnham expected to formally become Prime

— Mid-August: US/Iran: The 60-day ceasefire extension between the US and

— 29 Aug: Iceland referendum on EU accession talks.

the process to choose the next UN Secretary-General.
— 12-13 Sep: India will host the BRICS Summit in New Delhi.

— 13 Sep: German state and local elections.
— 13 Sep: Swedish general election.
— 18-20 Sep: Russian legislative election.
— 24 Sep: China’s President Xi is expected to visit the United States
following the China-US Summit in Beijing in May.
— 30 Sep: Key US government funding deadline. Failure to pass

Commodity markets
Oil was trading around $73/bbl, but prices are now rising back towards $80/bbl. Several transport-
and materials-exposed companies have pointed to 2027 for normalization (see here).

Oil: Under the ceasefire, we had placed Brent at $75/bbl
(Q3’26) and $70/bbl (Q4’26).
— Oil prices fell faster than expected during the MOU-interim,
although outlook is uncertain for now with the reignition of Strait
conflict.
— Crisis has revealed need for broader reserves beyond crude oil
and gas, where disruption can rapidly cascade through supply
chains and consumer prices (i.e. refined fuels, petrochemicals,
fertilizers, food, metals).
— Companies should now be expected to maintain larger

inventories, build in more redundancy, spend more on insurance,
hedging, and transport, and accept duplicated routes and
suppliers.
Gold: revised base case to reach $4,800/oz in Q4
— Tighter Fed monetary policy and resilient US macro data has
pushed gold prices lower.
Brent crude oil prices ($/bbl) have gone from around $100/bbl to just $73/bbl during MOU

14 Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Luke Templeman, Adrian Cox, Helen Beloposky, Miha Hribernik, Jim Reid | Macro, Markets, Geopolitics and Tech: A Guide for CFOs and Corporates | 13 July 2026
Rates & Credit markets
Despite higher rates, financial conditions are relatively accommodative, with credit spreads remaining
tighter than expected. Potential area for concern lies in private credit

Private credit is pricing in more stress now that full Q1 data is
in. There is potential for greater AI-disruption stress on
software and other adjacent subsectors.
— Software spreads are near Covid (March 2020) levels, but ex-
software spreads are trading much better. Market is pricing
software as an isolated issue, although there is a risk the stress will
spread more broadly over the next 6-12 months.
— Credit-spread forecasts slightly lower throughout 2026, with $IG

82bps; $HY 305bps. €IG 95bps; €HY 345bps.
Raised our YE-2026 targets for the 10yr US Treasury (4.80%)
and 10yr Bund (3.20%)
— Rationale: Expectations of more Fed hikes are now baked into our
forecast, while front loading of ECB hikes in response to the energy
shock has pushed our yield forecasts higher across the curve.
— Downside risks: AI tails from either a bubble or significant impact on
the market, or a concrete end to the Iran War leading to materially

lower oil prices.
— Upside risks: Demand for additional fiscal and capex globally or a
waning in foreign demand for US Treasuries that further lifts term
premia.
Reports: Global Fixed Income Weekly: Strategy Update - Deutsche Bank Research and Fixed Income Blog: EUR Rates Forecast
revision - Bund to 3.2% - Deutsche Bank Research and IG & HY Strategy: Global Default Monitor: Software woes, BDC selling
putting pressure on Loans - Deutsche Bank Research Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP.

Outlook until year-end calls for more dispersion and some spread widening
The ten year treasury yield initially traded in a tight correlation with oil prices, but that’s
completely opened up in the last month. Yields have stayed high even during the
ceasefire

15 Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Luke Templeman, Adrian Cox, Helen Beloposky, Miha Hribernik, Jim Reid | Macro, Markets, Geopolitics and Tech: A Guide for CFOs and Corporates | 13 July 2026
Foreign exchange markets
Hawkish FOMC is supporting the dollar
Hard to argue for significant dollar weakness
— The dollar has modestly benefited from rising energy prices, while

equity-led inflows into the US have also started to recover.
— Near-term, the second-order effects of the Iran War is dollar
supportive.
— However, the level of front-end US Treasury yields, the interest-
rate differential with the rest of the world and relative shape of the
US yield curve are not sending dollar bullish signals.
Currency outlook
— EUR/USD: 1.20 by YE (prevs 1.25). Escalation in Iran and more
hawkish repricing of the Fed could push EUR/USD below 1.15.

— EUR/JPY: Hawkish signals from BoJ officials, firm recent data, and
potential for repatriation sees the yen rising against the Euro.
Despite yen relatively flat against the Euro since our May blueprint,
we still expect the yen to strengthen in H2, with two potential
avenues: either the BoJ hikes rates to 2% more quickly than
expected, or the government undertakes a more concerted effort to
shift domestic investment back onshore.
— USD/CNH: Renminbi's appreciation will continue. Domestic

reflation, strong real-GDP growth, surpluses, and a greater use of
RMB in cross-border transactions/internationalisation should all act
as significant tailwinds.
Currency movements and DB forecasts for YE-2026 and 2027

Artificial intelligence
Companies are committing growing sums of capital to strengthen their AI capabilities
2025 was a breakout year for corporate AI investment

— Global AI corporate investment hit $582bn in 2025,
+130% from 2024, mostly driven by Gen AI.
(Source: McKinsey/Stanford).
— Private financing (VC/PE) was the largest share of
activity.
— The US continues to lead private AI investment;
China’s true total AI spending is understated, as
government guidance funds deploy a significant
amount.
The costs for AI are growing
— AI company revenues are rising at record pace –
but so are compute and infrastructure costs.

— Frontier-model token costs specifically have
become exponentially higher than still very
intelligent mid-tier models. This is one of the
biggest challenges for wide enterprise adoption.
— There is also evidence the AI-capex demand shock
is feeding into inflation, most visibly in computer
and semiconductor prices, specifically memory.
Global corporate investment in AI
The cost of tokens for frontier models is exponentially higher than still very intelligent mid-tier

models. This is one of the biggest challenges for widespread company adoption.

18 Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Luke Templeman, Adrian Cox, Helen Beloposky, Miha Hribernik, Jim Reid | Macro, Markets, Geopolitics and Tech: A Guide for CFOs and Corporates | 13 July 2026
Artificial intelligence
Job displacement from AI does not seem to be a near-term (~2 years) concern
Scaling AI adoption remains a challenge

— 88% of firms now use AI in at least one business function
(78% in 2024). Source: McKinsey/Stanford AI index
— Biggest y/y gains: Europe (+11pp to 91%) and China
(+13pp to 88%) (McKinsey/Stanford).
— Usage is highest in knowledge management for business,
legal, and professional services (58%), software
engineering (58%), and IT (56%).
— But scaling adoption lags: <10% of firms are scaling AI
agents across their firm.
AI job displacement not a near-term concern

— 56% of CEOs expect AI to have only a moderate impact
on their industries (Business Council and Conference
Board).
— Evidence so far shows little relationship between US
labour-market churn and AI adoption rate (see here).
— Yet, a third of firms expect AI to cut headcount in the
coming year – larger firms more so. The largest share
(43%) still expect little or no change. McKinsey/Stanford
Organizations are beginning to experiment with agentic AI, but diffusion is not yet

widespread
Majority of CEOs don’t think AI will fundamentally transform their firms, but most also
anticipate upskilling their workforce in the short term
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, McKinsey/Stanford Artificial Intelligence Index. Number of respondents = 1,933
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, The Conference Board Measure of CEO Confidence in collaboration with the Business Council, Q22026.
Left hand chart: Minimal impact (very few business processes will change; the industry remains largely unchanged); moderate impact (many

business processes will change, but the industry will not fundamentally transform); Significant impact (AI will affect nearlyevery business
process the industry will be fundamentally transformed.)

Asset Tokenization
— Transferable tokenized assets on-chain total $33bn (rwa.xyz).
— The SEC is  reportedly moving to permit trading of tokenized

stocks; ongoing DTCC progress on tokenized collateral
infrastructure.
— Several US banks have announced a tokenized-deposits clearing
network operated by The Clearing House, live from 2027 onwards,
with settlement in central bank reserves.
Stablecoins
— Adjusted stablecoin transaction volume is projected to reach
$719trn by 2035 (Chainalysis).
— Stablecoin payment volumes  could match Visa and Mastercard’s
off-chain transactions sometime between 2029-31.

CBDCs
— Wholesale: ECB’s Project Pontes – a settlement solution for DLT-
based transactions in central bank money – tracking for Sep 2026
launch.
— Retail: EP committee approved the digital euro framework June 23,
final law targeted end-2026. Pilot mid-2027; first issuance possible
2029.

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Miễn room tín dụng cho 18 dự 
án trọng điểm

Cơ chế tín dụng đặc thù cho 18 dự án trọng điểm của Vingroup, Sun Group và Masterise 
— Ngày 22/6/2026, NHNN ban hành Công văn số 5386/NHNN-TD hướng dẫn các NHTM 
cấp tín dụng cho 18 dự án trọng điểm theo kiến nghị của Vingroup, Sun Group và 
Masterise. Theo đó, NHNN cho phép các NHTM loại trừ dư phần nợ cấp cho các dự án 
này khỏi hạn mức tăng trưởng tín dụng và có thể xem xét nới hạn mức tín dụng được 
cấp cho một hoặc nhóm khách hàng có liên quan (theo Quyết định 09/2024/QĐ-TTg). 
— Danh mục dự án được đề xuất là các dự án hạ tầng giao thông chiến lược trong lĩnh vực 
hàng không, đường sắt tốc độ cao, và các công trình phục vụ APEC. Tổng vốn huy động 
của nhóm dự án này là 752.1 nghìn tỷ đồng, thực hiện trong giai đoạn 2026-2033, trong 
đó khoảng 85% tổng vốn huy động dự kiến sẽ được giải ngân trong 3 năm đầu.  
— Với giả định tăng trưởng tín dụng toàn hệ thống năm 2026 đạt 15% (tương đương tổng 
dư nợ tín dụng đạt 21,384 nghìn tỷ đồng), quy mô vay vốn của nhóm dự án dự kiến sẽ 
chiếm khoảng 1% tổng dư nợ tín dụng cho mỗi năm trong giai đoạn 2026-2028, ước 
tính đóng góp vào tăng trưởng tín dụng thêm khoảng 0.5-1%/năm trong giai đoạn này.  
— Theo KBSV, quyết định này của NHNN phản ánh: 
(1) Cơ chế hỗ trợ tín dụng của NHNN đang mang tính chọn lọc khi mức đóng góp 
vào tăng trưởng tín dụng toàn hệ thống chỉ ở mức vừa phải, qua đó giúp khơi 
thông nguồn vốn cho các dự án trọng điểm mà không làm thay đổi đáng kể 
định hướng điều hành tín dụng chung của NHNN. 
(2) Tác động thực tế nhiều khả năng sẽ tập trung ở một số ngân hàng tham gia tài 
trợ dự án thay vì toàn hệ thống, tạo dư địa tăng trưởng tín dụng đáng kể nhưng 
đồng thời làm gia tăng mức độ tập trung tín dụng, yêu cầu các ngân hàng phải 
siết chặt khâu quản trị rủi ro.

Tác động lên mặt bằng lãi suất 
— Chúng tôi cho rằng quyết định này của NHNN sẽ tạo sức ép nhất định lên việc gia tăng 
vốn huy động của các NHTM tham gia giải ngân trực tiếp, đặc biệt khi chênh lệnh giữa 
huy động tiền gửi và dư nợ tín dụng toàn nền kinh tế tiếp tục nới rộng kể từ cuối 2025 
khiến tỷ lệ LDR thuần toàn hệ thống tăng mạnh (Biểu đồ 2).  
— Dù vậy, áp lực lên hệ thống sẽ được giảm bớt khi NHNN chủ động nâng tỷ lệ vốn ngắn 
hạn cho vay trung dài hạn từ 30% lên 40%, đồng thời cho phép tính thêm 20% tiền gửi 
KBNN vào tỷ lệ LDR (theo Thông tư 25/2026/TT-NHNN). Ngoài ra, chúng tôi cũng không 
loại trừ khả năng NHNN sẽ có thêm các chính sách hỗ trợ để bổ sung nguồn vốn cho 
vay các dự án này. Do đó, chúng tôi cho rằng mặt bằng lãi suất huy động sẽ ổn định ở 
vùng hiện tại, thậm chí có thể bắt đầu xu hướng giảm trong quý 3 nếu huy động của 
toàn hệ thống có sự cải thiện và giải ngân đầu tư công tăng mạnh.

### Page 2

2

Tác động lên thị trường chứng khoán 
Chúng tôi cho rằng Công văn số 5386/NHNN-TD  sẽ tạo tác động tích cực lên các nhóm 
ngành bao gồm: 
— Ngành ngân hàng: Tác động tích cực được kỳ vọng tập trung ở nhóm ngân hàng có 
năng lực thu xếp vốn cho các dự án hạ tầng quy mô lớn, nền tảng huy động vốn vững 
chắc và chất lượng tài sản tốt, đặc biệt là nhóm ngân hàng quốc doanh và ngân hàng 
tư nhân có vốn lớn. Ngoài ra, các ngân hàng như TCB, STB, LPB kỳ vọng cũng sẽ có lợi 
thế nhờ mối quan hệ đã được thiết lập từ trước với Vingroup. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi 
không loại trừ rủi ro tập trung tín dụng vào một số NHTM có quan hệ gần với các chủ 
đầu tư có thể gây ảnh hưởng không nhỏ đến nguồn vốn và hiệu quả hoạt động của 
nhóm này.  
— Nhóm ngành xây lắp – vật liệu xây dựng: Cơ chế tín dụng đặc thù kỳ vọng sẽ hỗ trợ đẩy 
nhanh tiến độ triển khai và giải ngân dự án, qua đó cải thiện triển vọng doanh thu, 
backlog của các doanh nghiệp xây lắp cũng như thúc đẩy nhu cầu tiêu thụ vật liệu xây 
dựng trong các năm tới.

Biểu đồ 1. Vốn huy động của nhóm 18 dự án giai đoạn 2026-2033 và tỷ trọng trên tổng dư nợ 
tín dụng toàn hệ thống năm 2026

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets retraced much of the week’s earlier gains as
semiconductor stocks fell, with investors reassessing elevated
valuations across the AI sector. Oil’s advance stalled as both
sides continued to target military assets around the Strait,

with the US having earlier struck an Iranian-linked vessel
reportedly transiting towards Kharg Island. President Trump
has threatened to expand the target list to include Iran’s
critical infrastructure, with Tehran reportedly asking Houthi
rebels to respond by closing Red Sea oil trade routes should
such strikes occur.
• Equities softened as investors took profits in outperforming
AI-related stocks, despite strong quarterly earnings from
Taiwanese chipmaker TSMC. In the US, the S&P 500 fell

0.5%, led by a 1.5% decline in the NASDAQ, while the Dow
Jones posted a more modest fall of 0.2%. European equities
bucked the broader trend, with the Euro Stoxx 50 rising 0.3%
and the FTSE 100 gaining 0.5%.
• Asian markets also moved lower. Korea’s KOSPI declined
6.4% with authorities later announcing a temporary halt
on new listings of single-stock leveraged exchange-traded
products, which have contributed to the recent rise in market
volatility. The Nikkei 225 fell 2.8%, while the Hang Seng

outperformed, gaining 1.3%. Locally, the ASX 200 was
unchanged.
• US Treasuries were little changed overnight, with both
the 2-year and 10-year yields rising 1bp. Futures markets
continue to price an additional Fed hike this year, with
December seen as the most likely timing, taking the implied
year-end cash rate to 3.90%.
• Australian government bond yields were stable, with
consumer inflation expectations falling to their lowest
level since January. The 3-year yield declined 1bp, while

the 10-year yield was unchanged. Swap markets remain
unconvinced of an additional RBA hike, with the implied
year-end terminal cash rate at 4.52%.
• FX markets saw the USD partially reverse recent weakness,
with the DXY rising 0.2%. The AUD softened 0.1% to
0.6996, while the yen (-0.1%), euro (-0.2%) and sterling
(-0.5%) also weakened against the greenback. Across the
crosses, the AUD was little changed against the euro, yen
and NZD, while gaining 0.4% against sterling.

• Commodities saw the rally in oil prices stall for the first time
this week, with Brent easing 0.1% to US$84.9/bbl and WTI
unchanged at US$79.6/bbl. Gold continued to decline as
elevated oil prices renewed inflation concerns, falling 2.1%
to move back below US$4,000/oz. Copper rose 0.1%, while
aluminium gained 1.1% after Alcoa lowered its full-year
alumina production forecast following operational issues at
an Australian refinery. Iron ore declined 0.3%.

International Data
US retail sales met market expectations in June, headline
sales rising 0.2% in the month after a 1.0% gain in May, with
the control group up 0.5% following a 0.8% lift last month.
Consumer demand is below trend, but still growing at a
modest, positive pace despite historic uncertainty and elevated
borrowing costs.
US initial jobless claims declined at the margin last week, from
216k to 208k, a very low level versus history.
The US Philadelphia Fed manufacturing index jumped from

10.3 to 41.4 in July. The regional surveys remain highly volatile
month-to-month and differ significantly from the official data.
Fed speakers focused on inflation risks overnight. Kansas City
Fed President Schmid made clear inflation remains his biggest
concern. Dallas Fed President Logan called for higher interest
rates, believing that inflation is not heading sustainably back to
the FOMC’s medium-term target.

### Page 1

• Taiwan–Singapore is emerging as one of 
Asia’s fastest-growing trade and investment 
corridors. 
• AI-driven demand and the continued 
evolution of semiconductor supply chains 
are powering rapid growth. 
• Singapore’s role as a regional hub for trade, 
logistics, finance and business continues to 
accelerate corridor expansion. 
• Bilateral trade is on track to double this year, 
while two-way investment flows are 
expected to more than double. 
• Taiwan has become Singapore’s largest 
trading partner, while Singapore is poised to 
become Taiwan’s fourth -largest trading 
partner this year. 
• Singapore is on track to become Taiwan’s 
largest source of FDI and its second-
largest destination for ODI this year.

Taiwan-Singapore: Asia’s new 
growth corridor

1 July 2026

Ma Tieying, CFA                        
Senior Economist 
matieying@dbs.com

### Page 2

Taiwan-Singapore: Asia’s new growth corridor July 1, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

The Singapore–Taiwan corridor is emerging as 
one of Asia’s fastest -growing trade and 
investment relationships. The rapid expansion 
of the AI ecosystem, deeper semiconductor 
supply-chain integration and diversification, 
and Singapore’s growing role as a reg ional 
trade, logistics, finance and business hub are 
strengthening economic linkages between the 
two economies at an unprecedented pace.

Trade

Bilateral trade momentum strengthened 
sharply this year . Taiwan’s exports to 
Singapore surged 116.7% yoy during Jan–May 
to USD33.2 b n, while imports from Singapore 
increased 69.4% to USD8.6 bn. If current 
trends continue, Taiwan’s exports to Singapore 
could approach USD80 bn this year and 
imports from Singapore could reach USD20 bn, 
bringing total bilateral trade to roughly USD100 
bn. S ingapore would consequently become 
Taiwan’s fourth-largest trading partner, behind 
only the US, China, and South Korea.

From Singapore’s perspective, the relationship 
is even larger. Singapore’s trade statistics 
capture not only domestic exports and imports 
but also goods transshipped through the city -
state. On this basis, both Singapore’s exports 
to Taiwan and imports from  Taiwan are likely 
to exceed USD100 bn this year, implying total 
bilateral trade of more than USD200 bn. 
Taiwan has been Singapore’s largest trading 
partner since 2025 and is likely to retain that 
position through 2026.

### Page 3

Taiwan-Singapore: Asia’s new growth corridor July 1, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

A major driver of this growth is Singapore’s 
expanding role as a regional distribution 
hub for information and communications 
technology (ICT) products . World -class 
logistics infrastructure, extensive shipping and 
air-cargo connectivity, efficient customs 
procedures, and a tariff -free trading 
environment have made Singapore a preferred 
gateway for technology supply chains serving 
Southeast Asia, India, t he Middle East, and 
Europe.

The scale of re -export activity underscores 
Singapore’s intermediary role. Approximately 
75% of Singapore’s exports to Taiwan originate 
from third countries, while roughly half of its 
imports from Taiwan are subsequently re -
exported. As Taiwanese technolog y 
companies expand overseas, Singapore is 
becoming an increasingly important platform 
for distributing ICT products across the region.

Beyond its traditional role as a trading hub, 
Singapore is increasingly becoming a 
major source of end-demand for Taiwanese 
technology products . As Southeast Asia’s 
largest cloud-computing and data -centre hub, 
Singapore continues to attract substantial 
investment from hyperscalers and digital 
infrastructure providers , including AWS, 
Google, and Microsoft. Its stable regulatory 
environment, concentration of multinational 
headquarters, and extensive submarine cable 
network make Singapore one of Asia’s most 
attractive destinations for AI infrastructure 
investment.

Roughly half of Singapore’s imports from 
Taiwan are consumed domestically, reflecting 
strong local demand for AI servers, 
semiconductors, advanced packaging services, 
and other technology products in which Taiwan 
holds global leadership.

The development of the Johor –Singapore 
Special Economic Zone (JS -SEZ) further 
reinforces this trend. With greater land 
availability and lower power costs, Johor is 
attracting a growing pipeline of data-centre and 
advanced manufacturing investments. 
Singapore serve s as the primary logistics, 
supply-chain, and financing gateway for many 
of these projects, generating additional 
demand for AI servers, semiconductors, and 
other technology products sourced from 
Taiwan.

Deepening integration between 
Singapore’s and Taiwan’s semiconductor 
industries is another important driver of 
bilateral trade growth . Singapore has

### Page 4

Taiwan-Singapore: Asia’s new growth corridor July 1, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

become an increasingly important 
manufacturing and operational hub within the 
global semiconductor ecosystem. Micron 
operates NAND flash production facilities in 
Singapore and is developing advanced  HBM 
packaging capabilities there, while Applied 
Materials is expanding its semiconductor 
equipment manufacturing footprint.

At the same time, Taiwan continues to 
strengthen its position across  advanced logic 
chip manufacturing, DRAM/HBM production, 
and advanced packaging services. Micron is 
increasing DRAM/HBM production capacity in 
Taiwan, while leading Taiwanese 
semiconductor firms continue to invest heavily 
in advanced foundry processes and advanced 
packaging capabilities.

Trade patterns reflect this growing 
complementarity. Taiwan’s exports to 
Singapore are concentrated in electronic 
components (54%) and information and 
communication products (30%), while imports 
from Singapore are dominated by electronic 
components (32%), machinery (32%), and 
optical and precision instruments (13%).

Investment

The Singapore–Taiwan investment corridor 
is expanding as rapidly as trade . Taiwan’s 
approved foreign direct investment ( FDI) 
inflows from Singapore surged nearly sixteen -
fold yoy to USD5.3 bn during Jan –May, while 
Taiwan’s approved outbound investment (ODI) 
to Singapore increased by a similar magnitude 
to USD1.0 bn. Singapore’s investment into 
Taiwan could exceed USD7 bn this year, while 
Taiwan’s investment into Singapore could 
surpass USD2 bn, bringing total bilateral 
investment flows to around USD10 bn. O n a 
single-country basis, Singapore is on track to 
become Taiwan’s largest source of FDI and 
second-largest destination for outbound 
investment after the US.

The relationship appears even stronger when 
viewed through Singapore’s investment 
statistics, which capture investments routed 
through third jurisdictions and regional holding 
structures. On this basis, Singapore’s ODI into 
Taiwan could reach approximately  USD7 bn 
this year, while Taiwanese FDI into Singapore 
could approach USD6 bn, implying total 
bilateral investment flows comfortably 
exceeding USD10 bn.

### Page 5

Taiwan-Singapore: Asia’s new growth corridor July 1, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

Singapore’s growing importance as a 
regional business hub is a major factor 
supporting investment growth . An 
increasing number of multinational 
corporations and Taiwanese firms use 
Singapore as their headquarters for Southeast 
Asian and broader Asia -Pacific operations. 
The city -state offers a competitive tax 
framework, strong legal protections, and a 
highly business-friendly operating environment.

Singapore is also Taiwan’s most important 
institutional economic partner in Asia, linked 
through the ASTEP economic partnership 
agreement, which closely resembles a free -
trade agreement. As Taiwanese companies 
continue to internationalise, Singapore is 
becoming an increasingly important base for  
supply-chain management , regional treasury 
centres, and investment holding structures.

Recent transactions illustrate this trend. In April, 
Google invested USD855 mn through its 
Singapore subsidiaries to support data -centre 
and semiconductor procurement activities in 
Taiwan. In May, Micron injected USD4.3 bn 
through its Singapore holding com pany to 
expand operations in Taiwan. In the opposite 
direction, Foxconn invested USD350 mn 
through Singapore to support manufacturing 
expansion in Vietnam.

AI-related investment is becoming one of 
the most important drivers of bilateral 
capital flows . As AI adoption accelerates, 
demand for AI infrastructure, semiconductors, 
and other technology hardware is rising rapidly 
in Singapore, Taiwan, and their major trading 
partners.

Recent investments by firms such as Google 
and Micron in Taiwan provide clear examples 
of this trend. Taiwanese companies are also 
expanding their presence in Singapore’s AI 
infrastructure ecosystem. A notable example is 
BizLink’s USD850 mn acquisition of 
Singapore-based Interplex Datacom,

strengthening its position in data -centre 
connectivity and AI-related infrastructure.

Semiconductor supply-chain 
diversification strategies driven by 
geopolitical considerations provide an 
additional structural tailwind. As Taiwanese 
semiconductor companies seek more 
geographically diversified and resilient 
production networks, Singapore has emerged 
as a preferred destination owing to its political 
stability, strategic neutrality , and well -
established semiconductor ecosystem.

Several Taiwanese semiconductor firms have 
expanded their presence in Singapore in 
recent years , extending beyond AI -related 
activities. UMC and Vanguard have invested in 
mature-node semiconductor manufacturing 
facilities, while King Yuan established its first 
overseas semiconductor testing operation in 
the city-state.

Conclusion

The Singapore–Taiwan economic relationship 
is evolving beyond a traditional trad e and 
investment partnership into a strategically 
important technology corridor centred on AI, 
semiconductors, and regional supply -chain 
management. Continued expansion of AI 
infrastructure, deeper semiconductor supply -
chain integration and diversification, and 
Singapore’s role as Asia’s leading trade  and 
business hub are likely to drive sustained 
growth in bilateral trade and investment flows. 
As the two economies  become increasingly 
interconnected, their partnership is emerging 
as a critical node within Asia’s evolving 
technology networks.

Document 357: The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem (2026-07-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem (2026-07-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-07-16
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank
Research
From Panda and Dim Sum Bonds to Deeper Capital Markets
The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem

### Page 2

Perry Kojodjojo | Chen Kan |  The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem |  July 20261
Key takeaways
– RMB’s global ambition. China is actively shifting RMB from one used for trade to a global investment and financing currency. The
Panda (CNY bonds issued by foreign entities within China)  and Dim Sum (CNH bonds issued in Hong Kong) bond markets are
the core pillars of this strategy, creating the necessary ecosystem for the RMB's international expansion.

– Panda bonds - Unlocking onshore China for global use. The onshore Panda bond market has matured into a strategic funding
tool for foreign issuers. Thanks to regulatory easing on fund usage, over half of the proceeds are now used offshore, making it a
flexible funding instrument and cementing the role of foreign participants who now account for over 30% of issuance.
– Dim Sum bonds - A deepening offshore hub. The offshore Dim Sum market is experiencing a surge in sophistication and

diversity, signaling its evolution from a policy-driven market into a mature offshore funding hub. It is now driven by new issuers
like tech firms, is offering longer-term bonds, and is set to receive a massive demand boost from new regulations allowing
mainland insurers to invest in the market.
– Next frontier - a focus on building a complete financial ecosystem. While both markets are growing, challenges remain, including
broadening the issuer base beyond financial institutions. The key to addressing these challenges is building a complete RMB

financing ecosystem, supported by deep secondary-market liquidity, reliable benchmark yield curves for price discovery, and
comprehensive risk-management tools. Ultimately, this is a gradual but foundational journey to establish the RMB as a more
widely funded, invested, and held international currency.
– Expanding European participation in RMB funding markets. While European entities dominate foreign issuance in both Panda
and Dim Sum markets, this participation is highly concentrated among a few financial sector repeat borrowers, with corporates

largely absent. Barriers include smaller deal sizes, a narrower investor base, and lack of market familiarity. As RMB
internationalization progresses, we anticipate broader international issuer participation, particularly from European corpora tes
given strengthening EU-China ties.

### Page 4

Perry Kojodjojo | Chen Kan |  The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem |  July 20263
RMB Internationalization: A New Chapter Driven by Diverging Bond
Markets
China's policy stance on RMB internationalization has shifted from cautious to explicit , with the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026-30)
elevating it to a strategic priority. This shift is supported by a series of concrete actions in early 2026 amidst a global de-dollarization
trend. However, progress remains uneven. While there is strong official commitment, evidenced by record transaction volumes on

China's Cross-Border Interbank Payment System (CIPS) and a surge in RMB goods trade settlement to a projected 30% by 2025,
the currency's broader global footprint is still modest. The RMB holds only a 1-4% share across global payments, FX reserves, and
debt securities, which is disproportionately low compared to China's approximately 19% share of global GDP.
A fundamental change has occurred in the nature of cross-border RMB usage. The center of gravity has moved from trade to

investment flows, with capital and financial account transactions now constituting around 75% of total RMB settlements. This
means the next phase of internationalization requires building a deeper and more accessible financial ecosystem for raising a nd
deploying RMB capital globally. This includes creating more robust channels for funding, such as the onshore Panda and offshore
Dim Sum bond markets, alongside more sophisticated hedging tools and a broader array of attractive RMB-denominated assets.

Since 2025, these two key bond markets have entered a new, more dynamic chapter, accelerating on different tracks and
developing distinct characteristics. The onshore Panda bond market is experiencing a record year, with issuance in the first half of
2026 exceeding RMB160bn. This boom is driven by fundamental improvements to its architecture, including streamlined issuance
procedures and more flexible rules that allow issuers to remit proceeds overseas. This has transformed Panda bonds from a ring-

fenced currency pot into a source of globally usable RMB funding.
In contrast, the offshore Dim Sum market’s strong growth is driven by demand diversification. It is poised for a significant boost as
mainland insurers gain access via the Southbound Bond Connect, whose quota has been expanded to RMB 800bn, creating a new
source of demand for an increasingly foreign-led issuer base.
The markets are also evolving with unique "DNA." The Panda market's issuer base remains dominated by traditional sectors like

financials, while the Dim Sum market is diversifying and embracing tech. Issuance from IT giants has risen sharply in the Dim Sum
market as they seek cheap, long-tenor funding for their AI and cloud capex cycles. This reflects a difference in their duration profiles
as well. The Panda market remains overwhelmingly short-term, with nearly all issuance at three years or less due to bank-dominated
demand and a flat yield curve. Conversely, the Dim Sum market is showing clear maturity with a growing share of bonds in the 5-

year and longer tenors, which better aligns with the investment horizons of the tech sector.

The Panda bond market's record issuance year, even without a clear cost advantage, confirms its shift from a tactical choice to a
strategic funding tool. This maturation is driven by two key structural changes:
– From onshore tool to global funding. Regulatory easing has been a game-changer. Over half of Panda bond proceeds (53%) are

now used offshore, making it a flexible, globally-integrated RMB funding instrument.
– From niche to mainstay. Foreign issuers are now a cornerstone of the market, consistently accounting for over 30% of issuance
and showing early signs of diversification beyond just financial institutions.
While the market remains concentrated in short-term debt (<3 years) and dominated by domestic bank investors, the modest return
of foreign buyers is a positive signal for future market depth.

Share of gross issuance (12MMA, %)
CN HK Foreign
The offshore Dim Sum market is undergoing a structural transformation, becoming deeper and more sophisticated. Three key

trends define its new trajectory.
– Issuer base revolution. The market is now driven by new participants. Foreign entities have become a cornerstone, accounting for
over 25% of issuance. Domestically, high-growth Tech/IT companies have decisively replaced real estate as the primary issuers,
reflecting growing demand for long-duration RMB funding to support AI, cloud and digital infrastructure investment.
– Market maturation. Issuance tenors are extending significantly. The growing share of bonds with 5+ year maturities, and the

emergence of a nascent 15Y+ market, signal rising investor confidence and more sophisticated funding strategies.
– Demand catalysts unlocked. Recent policy changes are a game-changer for demand. Allowing mainland insurers to buy Dim Sum
bonds via Southbound Bond Connect, alongside an increased quota (to RMB800bn) and wider product scope, is set to unlock a
massive and sustainable new pool of capital.
In short, the Dim Sum market is evolving from a niche offshore CNH pool into a more mature, diversified, and institutionally

supported market with a powerful new demand base on the horizon. Together, these developments are helping to establish the
foundations of a deeper and more liquid offshore RMB capital market.

### Page 11

Perry Kojodjojo | Chen Kan |  The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem |  July 202610
Building the offshore RMB ecosystem
Over the next five years, Panda and Dim Sum bonds are set to be the two core pillars driving the RMB’s evolution from a trade-
settlement currency into a global investment and financing currency . Together, these markets are creating a comprehensive
ecosystem that provides the essential assets for international players to fund, invest, and manage RMB holdings.

The Panda bond market, where foreign entities issue RMB-denominated debt inside mainland China, is successfully transitioning
from a policy initiative to a viable funding channel. Regulatory reforms have improved access for a diverse range of international
issuers, leading to growing market confidence and projected annual volume growth of 20-30%. However, significant hurdles remain,
including a narrow investor base, a lack of robust tools for hedging interest rate risk, and complex issuance rules that need further

standardization.
Concurrently, the Dim Sum bond market, consisting of RMB bonds issued outside mainland China (primarily in Hong Kong), is
solidifying its position as the primary offshore RMB hub. The focus here is on building a reliable benchmark yield curve through
regular government issuance and upgrading market infrastructure with enhancements to Bond Connect and innovations like digital
bonds. This market's growth, however, remains highly sensitive to offshore RMB liquidity, currency expectations, and overall

investor demand. Its long-term success depends on creating a deep and sustainable ecosystem rather than just growing issuance
volume.
Ultimately, the development of these bond markets is a gradual but foundational journey . Much like the "Samurai" market was for
the internationalization of the Japanese Yen, Panda and Dim Sum bonds are creating the necessary building blocks for the RMB's
global future. While this evolution is subject to broader factors such as capital account policies, market reforms, and the geopolitical

landscape, the strategic goal is clear: to establish a robust foundation for the RMB to be more widely funded, invested, and held
across the world.

### Page 13

Perry Kojodjojo | Chen Kan |  The Rise of the RMB Financing Ecosystem |  July 202612
EU issuers account for a large share of foreign RMB bond
issuance…
…but the issuances are overwhelmingly concentrated in financial
institutions
Expanding European participation in RMB funding markets
European entities are major players in the RMB bond market, representing over half of foreign Panda and a third of Dim Sum
issuance. However, the issuer base is highly concentrated, with financial institutions dominating (85-95%) and repeat borrowers

accounting for over 90% of activity. The key challenge lies in attracting new issuers, not retaining existing ones.
Primary hurdles include smaller deal sizes compared to major markets, a narrower investor base, and lack of market familiarity.
These factors often make the upfront investment in RMB funding infrastructure, documentation, and investor relationships
economically unfeasible.
Looking ahead, we anticipate these barriers will ease with RMB internationalization. A deeper offshore RMB ecosystem will support

larger benchmark issuance, broaden the investor base, and improve liquidity. Further development of CNH derivatives markets will
simplify currency risk hedging and RMB proceeds swaps. Additionally, China's low interest rates and growing China-related
business exposure could enhance RMB funding appeal for international corporates. As the cost-benefit equation improves, we
expect future market development to be driven by a broader range of international issuers, particularly European corporates, rather

than larger issuances from existing borrowers.

規模資料中心發債應大多已完成 ，且另一主要產
業銀行的發債有提前至上半年的跡象 ，意即投資
級債下半年發債規模應不如上半年 ，對於技術面
更為有利。

《美國公司債發行人出現初步K型分化的跡象》
相較於投資級債 ，財務緩衝較差的非投資級債發行
人，在高通膨以及不如預期寬鬆的貨幣政策之下基
本面將承受更大壓力；儘管美國企業資產負債表大
致仍健康 ，但改善趨勢在投資級債發行人更為顯著 ，
這個差異主要來自於獲利能力的分化 ，導致美國公
司債發行人也出現初步K型分化的跡象。
在歐洲方面雖然總體經濟狀況明顯不如美國 ，但企

業基本面也有所反彈 ，因歐洲債市尤其是投資級債
其實包含大量國外發行人 (尤其是美國大型企業 )，
這也讓歐洲債信體質在面臨風險事件時或是通膨衝
BCA以獲利能力 、槓桿率與流動性等指標評估企業
債信體質與信用利差的關係 ，結果顯示具一定相關
性，故預期有獲利支撐 、基本面較為穩健的投資級
債信用利差下半能應能持續維持在偏低水準。
另外從技術面來看 ，投資級債也更為有優勢 ，首先
因為無風險利率上升推動收益型需求 ，收益型投資
人以退休基金 、保險公司為主更傾向投資等級債券 ，
再來是近年因債券型 ETF的興起 ，美國投資級債市
場流動性有顯著改善 ，流動性溢價縮減；上述皆有
助於信用利差的穩定程度。
資料來源：BCA，2026/6，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/6

重點摘要
• 債務規模擴大的企業同時獲
利有所成長 ，較高的利率環
境也不太會抑制企業透過增
加債務以支持未來獲利的意
願
• 因獲利成長 ，信用指標如槓
桿率與利息保障倍數等重點
觀察項目惡化的可能性也不
高
《非投資級債內部基本面也出現了K型分化》
雖然不及投資級債發行人亮眼，1Q26美國非投資級
債發行人獲利和獲利展望普遍優於市場預期 ，但部
分信用指標有所下滑 ，最值得關注的是槓桿率上升 ，
且利息覆蓋率下降 ，這兩個指標分別比長期平均高
出0.26倍和低0.43倍(摩根大通估計)，主要原因為總
債務成長加速至疫情後新高 (年增6.6%)，主要仍是
再融資需求所推動 ，以及發行人再融資成本更高導
致利息成本上升；另一個值得注意的觀察點是美非

投資級債利息覆蓋率超過 5倍的比例達7個季度以來
的最高水準 (42%)，而償付能力低於 2倍的比例達 5
年高點(22%)，反映K型分化在非投資級債當中也已
現蹤。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《高利率對不同評等非投資級債發行人影響不一致 》
美國公司債今年目前為止的發行量皆高於去年同期 ，
投資級債主要受資本支出需求推動 ，而這些多為高
評等(A以上)的企業，所以對於指數結構來說影響相
對正面；非投資級債主要受再融資需求推動 ，隨著
2028債務到期相對高點漸近 ，預期將持續推動非投
資級債發行，不過年初至今的發行中約有 56%為BB
級債券，與去年持平，CCC級發行債券的比例僅3%，
創歷史新低 ，對整指數結構也算正面效果；與過去
幾年相比 ，擔保債券也更受發行人青睞 ，年初至今
的發行中擔保債券佔比為48%，高於2024年的44%
和2025年的37%，反映利率維持在相對高位，發行
人以擔保品來降低發債利率 ，故擔保債券佔比上升

的趨勢可能持續 。另一方面雖然到期牆推動非投資
級債再融資需求 ，但不同評級的債券影響並不一致 ，
BB級和B級債券目前市場利率與平均票面利率之間
的差距接近零 ，這意味著再融資成本極低 ，不易對
發行人財務狀況造成太大壓力 ，但對於CCC級債券
的市場利率仍然與票面利率有相當差距 ，反映再融
資壓力將明顯高於其他評等的發行人。
本行預期高利率將維持一段時間 ，因此對發行人利
息成本的壓力仍將持續 ，發行人的選擇將會是非投
資級債的投資重點；據高盛估計 ，假設違約損失率
與歷史平均相當 ，美國非投資級債 B等級信用風險
溢價是三個評級當中最低 ，雖然CCC信用風險溢價
最高但在高利率持續下基本面進一步惡化的風險也
較大，綜合評估建議美非投資級債優先考慮基本面
較穩健，且風險溢價較為合理的BB等級。
4
高利率之下的再融資壓力對於低評等發行人更為衝擊
資料來源：巴克萊，2026/6；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6
美非投資級債 市場利率與平均票面利率之差%

接近零 ，這意味著再融資成

### Page 1

*：舉例說明，非單一標的推薦

台北富邦銀行 
July 8，2026 | 4:00 PM (GMT+8) 
主要產業 總評： 玻璃橋 挑戰 CPO
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎光纖和晶片溝通的障礙 
康寧*在 6/24 日技術論壇上再次分享去年 9 月發布過
的技術突破紀錄，要理解這次事件，得先知道 AI 晶片
內部的光通訊是怎麼運作的 。 資料在晶片裡是用電訊號
跑，但要傳到很遠的地方，就得先轉換成光訊號，透過
光纖傳輸，這個轉換的關鍵零件叫光子積體電路※，也
就是 PIC。 問題是 ， 光纖本身很粗 ， 直徑大約125 微米 ，
但 PIC 上面的光波導入口卻窄得多 ， 兩者尺寸差了好幾
倍，硬要對接，光訊號會嚴重衰減，這種現象業界稱為
模場失配※。 
過去業界解決這個問題，主要靠光纖陣列單元，也就是
FAU※。做法是把好幾根光纖固定在一塊有溝槽的基板
上，經過拋光、對準後，再一根一根接到晶片上，這套
工法已經很成熟，客製化彈性也高，損耗表現優異，但
缺點是隨著晶片上需要接的光纖數量越多 ， 組裝的複雜
度與成本就會直線上升 ， 對於下一代高密度的共封裝光
學系統來說，逐漸成為量產的瓶頸。 
如果把晶片內部比喻為車道極窄 、 高密度的市區路網 ，
外部光纖是寬闊的高速公路，那麼  GlassBridge 就像
是一座完美的交流道 ， 負責將密集的微小光訊號車流平
順地展開並無損地引導至大車道 ， 確保兩邊高速接軌而
不塞車流失。 
▎康寧光纖與晶片之間，加玻璃橋 
康寧這次推出的 GlassBridge，簡單來說就是在光纖和
晶片中間，插入一塊經過特殊處理的玻璃元件，扮演緩
衝與轉接的角色。這塊玻璃內部利用離子交換 ※技術，
在晶圓層級就先做出精密的光波導管道 ， 等於把過去要
靠人工逐一對準的工序， 改成用晶圓製程一次大量做好 ，
理論上有機會提升良率並降低成本。 
圖 1 GlassBridge 光纖至光子積體電路 (PIC) 連接器

資料來源：康寧 北富銀彙整  2026/6 
※光子積體電路(PIC) 在晶片上直接做出光學電路的元件，負責處理
光訊號的產生、調變與接收。 
※模場失配 光纖與晶片內部波導的光束尺寸不一致，導致訊號在對
接時產生額外損耗的現象。 
◼ 近期康寧*在首爾舉辦的 AI 資料中心光通訊與互連技術會議上，正式推出了一項
名為 GlassBridge 的玻璃光學連接器技術，市場隨即開始討論，這項新技術是否
會顛覆現有光纖陣列單元※廠商的市場地位，其切入光纖對接晶片的關鍵環節，
並已納入其 100 億美元光子學業務目標當中，然而實際商業化時程仍不明確。 
◼ GlassBridge 主打的是解決光纖與光子晶片對接時的密度與良率問題，這正是共
封裝光學※技術要大規模量產前，必須先跨過的一道工程門檻。GlassBridge 同
時適用於共封裝光學與近封裝光學兩種架構，加上台積電規劃逐步擴充光子晶片
產能，短期內對收發器廠商的實際影響有限，市場對相關股票的預期已偏低。  
◼ 長期來看，傳統 FAU 廠商確實面臨技術被部分取代的風險，但具備高階客製化
能力與量產經驗的既有龍頭廠，仍可望持續主導市場，投資人應留意技術演進速
度，而非直接視為產業末日。

### Page 2

2 
*：舉例說明，非單一標的推薦 
誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
※光纖陣列單元(FAU) 將多根光纖固定並排列整齊的精密零件 ， 負責
把外部光纖接到晶片上的光學入口。 
這項技術有幾個特點值得留意。第一是密度更高，單一
個連接器寬度只有 6.4 毫米，就能塞入 24 芯光纖，未
來還能往更高密度擴展 。 第二是可拆卸重工 ， 傳統FAU
一旦黏合固定就很難拆下重修，GlassBridge 採用可插
拔的標準介面，維修彈性較好。第三是製程相容性佳，
康寧表示這項技術支援迴焊製程※，比較容易與現有電
子封裝流程整合。不過也要提醒，GlassBridge 目前主
要應用在邊緣耦合※這種一維光纖排列的場景，還沒辦
法完全取代所有 FAU 的應用型態 ， 因此外資普遍認為 ，
這是重新分配製造責任， 而非直接取代既有解決方案 。 
※迴焊製程 常見的電子零件焊接方式，透過加熱讓零件與電路板上
的錫膏熔合固定。 
※邊緣耦合 光訊號從晶片側邊進出的對接方式，相對於從晶片表面
垂直進出的耦合方式。 
圖 2 GlassBridge 邊緣耦合局部放大

資料來源：康寧 北富銀彙整  2026/6 
▎玻璃橋仿可插拔技術 
在先進的晶片封裝架構中 ， 光訊號的傳遞就像走在一條
精心設計的捷徑 ： 訊號會先從外部的 MT 連接頭進入 ，  
圖 3 兩階段光學匹配機制

資料來源：ytyuted 北富銀彙整  2026/6 
通過康寧研發的 Glass Bridge 引導，最後精準地傳送
到核心的矽光子晶片 。 為了確保傳輸過程不漏光且訊號
穩定，連接頭內部特別設計了柔性彈簧，在對接時提供
持續且穩定的壓力 ， 讓光學端面之間達到完美的緊密貼
合，這不僅能降低訊號損耗，還能像避震器一樣吸收組
裝誤差或環境溫差帶來的衝擊 ， 防止零件因硬擠而受損 。
這項技術最關鍵的突破 ， 在於改變了過去必須用膠水把
光纖永久黏死在晶片邊緣的做法 ， 改為利用精密的金屬
導引針來對位 ， 讓原本極度困難的精密連接轉變為類似
「可插拔」 的標準化介面 ， 在維持封裝高精準度的同時 ，
也讓生產與組裝變得更加靈活高效。 
圖 4 玻璃橋仿可插拔技術

資料來源：康寧 北富銀彙整  2026/6 
▎CPO 供應鏈 
要判斷這次技術演進會如何影響投資布局 ， 需要先搞懂
整條供應鏈的分工。 
上游 光學材料與元件

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
材料，以及傳統 FAU 廠商如 TFC*、上詮*、Senko*、
波若威*與住友電工*，這些公司長期供應電信與資料中
心市場，具備成熟的精密製造能力。此外，鏡頭模組廠
商如大立光*，若切入 FAU 相關的透鏡元件，也有機會
受惠。 
中游 晶圓代工與晶片設計 
台積電是這個環節的關鍵角色 ， 負責製造光子積體電路 ，
公司規劃將 PIC 產能由 2026 年第 2 季的每月 1 萬片 ，
逐步提升到 2026 年第 4 季的 1.5 萬片，並在 2028 年
達到 2.5 萬片以上。主要晶片設計客戶包括輝達、博通
與超微，未來 Ayar Labs*、聯發科*、美滿電子*等公司
也有機會加入量產行列。 
下游 系統整合與封裝測試 
這個環節負責把光學元件 、 晶片與交換器整合成完整系
統，代表廠商包括日月光 *等封測業者，以及系統整合
商，最終產品交付給雲端服務供應商用於建置 AI 資料
中心。 
▎GlassBridge 不是 FAU 廠商的末日 
部分市場觀點認為 ，GlassBridge 一出來 ， 傳統FAU 廠
商的生意就會被快速取代 ，這種擔憂其實有點言過其實 。
從量產時程來看，外資調查顯示，台積電目前主流的
COUPE 平台，以及輝達、超微等主要客戶接下來幾年
的方案，仍會採用光柵耦合※這種較容易大量生產的技
術路徑，預計在 2026 年下半年才會進入放量階段。共
封裝光學牽涉到晶圓代工、晶片設計、光纖元件、雷射
光源與系統整合商等一整條生態系的協同設計 ， 要一夕
之間換掉某個環節的技術方案 ， 並不是一件容易的事 。 
※光柵耦合 讓光訊號從晶片表面垂直方向進出的對接技術，目前是
主流量產路徑。 
從個別廠商的競爭位置來看，具備高階 FAU 製造能力
的廠商，例如 TFC*，由於在精密度與量產經驗上仍有
優勢，GlassBridge 初期比較可能先取代技術門檻較低
的 FAU 應用，衝擊相對有限。至於以透鏡元件為主的
廠商，如果GlassBridge 進一步整合更多光學功能，確
實可能面臨較大的競爭壓力 ， 但如果廠商本身也具備完
整 FAU 系統的製造能力，則需要展現出對比既有玩家
的競爭力，才能維持市場份額。整體而言，外資對亞洲
共封裝光學相關供應鏈仍維持樂觀看法 ， 認為目前市場
預期已經偏低，包括台積電*、日月光*、FOCI*等公司
都被列入主要供應鏈名單。

大，或許比誰分到哪一塊更值得關注。 
▎參考半導體相關 ETF 
對於一般投資人來說 ， 要精準判斷單一新技術對個股的
長期影響，難度不低，比較穩健的做法是透過涵蓋整條
供應鏈的主題型 ETF 參與趨勢。例如 VanEck 半導體
ETF(SMH)，該 ETF 持有輝達與博通等在 AI 晶片與光
通訊晶片領域具領導地位的公司 ， 能涵蓋從運算晶片到
光學互連的完整價值鏈。那斯達克100 ETF(QQQ)則涵
蓋主要科技巨頭 ， 隨著資料中心持續導入新一代光學封
裝架構 ， 相關基礎設施升級需求可望帶動整體科技產業
